===== /tmp/cd/ctl/postinst
#!/bin/sh
# Debian maintainer script — runs as root via dpkg.
#
# Does three things on `configure`:
#   1. Writes an unconfined AppArmor profile so Chromium's namespace sandbox
#      works on Ubuntu 24.04+ (userns restriction).
#   2. Writes the Anthropic apt repository GPG key and source entry, so a
#      direct-download install becomes apt-managed and picks up updates on
#      the user's `apt upgrade` (the VS Code / Chrome / 1Password model),
#      plus a site-scoped unattended-upgrades Origins-Pattern snippet so
#      systems with unattended-upgrades enabled pick releases up
#      automatically.
#   3. Registers the GNOME Shell search provider (an .ini for the shell and
#      a D-Bus .service that starts the bundled GJS script on demand).
#
# Override the default with CLAUDE_DESKTOP_ADD_REPO="true"|"false" in
# /etc/default/claude-desktop. Only that one key is read from the file.
set -e

# DPKG_ROOT is set by dpkg for chrooted/cross installs; honouring it also
# lets the vitest in build/linux/maintainer-scripts.test.ts exercise this
# script against a scratch root without real root.
ROOT="${DPKG_ROOT:-}"

PROFILE_NAME="claude-desktop"
APPARMOR_DIR="$ROOT/etc/apparmor.d"
PROFILE="$APPARMOR_DIR/$PROFILE_NAME"
BINARY="/usr/lib/claude-desktop/claude-desktop"

# GNOME Shell search provider: a D-Bus-activated GJS script shipped under
# resources/ with its two registration files. Keep paths in sync with postrm
# and rpm-scripts.sh.
SEARCH_PROVIDER_DIR="$ROOT/usr/lib/claude-desktop/resources/gnome-search-provider"
SEARCH_PROVIDER_INI="$ROOT/usr/share/gnome-shell/search-providers/com.anthropic.Claude.search-provider.ini"
SEARCH_PROVIDER_SERVICE="$ROOT/usr/share/dbus-1/services/com.anthropic.Claude.SearchProvider.service"

write_search_provider() {
  mkdir -p "$(dirname "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_INI")" "$(dirname "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_SERVICE")"
  rm -f "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_INI" "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_SERVICE"  # see write_apt_keyring
  cp "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_DIR/com.anthropic.Claude.search-provider.ini" "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_INI"
  cp "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_DIR/com.anthropic.Claude.SearchProvider.service" "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_SERVICE"
  chmod 0644 "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_INI" "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_SERVICE"
}

# --- apt repository registration --------------------------------------------
# URL + suite per the Linux publishing spec
# (https://linux-publishing-spec.orbit.antspace.dev § Flow 2): the apt repo
# lives at the claude-desktop/apt/stable/ prefix of the prod bucket behind
# downloads.claude.ai, serving a signed InRelease since the 2026-07 launch.
# Registration is unconditional (admins opt out via CLAUDE_DESKTOP_ADD_REPO);
# taking the repo down follows the yank runbook in
# readme/desktop/linux-codesigning.md, not a packaging change.
APT_REPO_URL="https://downloads.claude.ai/claude-desktop/apt/stable"
APT_SUITE="stable"
APT_COMPONENT="main"
APT_KEYRING="$ROOT/usr/share/keyrings/claude-desktop-archive-keyring.asc"
APT_SOURCES="$ROOT/etc/apt/sources.list.d/claude-desktop.list"
APT_UNATTENDED="$ROOT/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/50claude-desktop"
APT_DEFAULT="$ROOT/etc/default/claude-desktop"
# Marker on the first line of the sources file we manage. If a sources file
# exists without this marker, the user (or their sysadmin) owns it and we
# don't touch it. Keep in sync with postrm.
APT_SOURCES_MARKER="### Managed by the claude-desktop package."

# Public key for the repo's InRelease signature (signed-by= scopes trust to
# this repo only — never global apt trust).
#
# Anthropic Claude Code Release Signing <security@anthropic.com>
# Fingerprint: 31DD DE24 DDFA B679 F42D 7BD2 BAA9 29FF 1A7E CACE
#
# This is the committed scripts/desktop/linux-signing/kms-gpg-signer/claude-code.asc;
# maintainer-scripts.test.ts asserts the two copies stay byte-identical.
write_apt_keyring() {
  mkdir -p "$(dirname "$APT_KEYRING")"
  # rm first so `cat >` creates a fresh regular file instead of writing
  # through a pre-existing symlink — in a DPKG_ROOT install that symlink
  # is target-rootfs content and could point at a host path. Parent-dir
  # symlinks are not defended: dpkg's own unpack step follows them too,
  # so a hostile rootfs can already clobber host paths regardless of
  # what this script does.
  rm -f "$APT_KEYRING"
  cat > "$APT_KEYRING" <<'CLAUDE_DESKTOP_PGP_EOF'
-----BEGIN PGP PUBLIC KEY BLOCK-----

mQINBGnK73ABEACnbytJXkjweYrwIr0aLEFRlH+C0nF44KxFc7gQmJ6PjSPMGZAD
dxZcaixU7zZl8WxEpVO0wLmIH8cf2zGOdyuZg1Yaugk1vHb2b8WBhAGCQJdPgB8W
XquedepEYtk56uP/gCoTjJDUZluEGBHnlnuujSJ4orxEdhSykEoAUfJZGEILPpMd
bphFt/Sn+Eb/TxM5jpKPdwnv8AShNF/1mZU1fWTQq9tRKJUakZj04gdaDFElQXak
CtTij+GT6yoYCARSHwGO+PC/Pr6q4tc+D7LRjxSBvUWDoFSmlqb/PJ1hj9D/7I2O
e4XXniAPWMR56KvxHlzOzrNQdJujbJdSkCwh1ZijkSd3y8ayW5WYUTGdRab99NUw
agzlabe/VVF6kzJ0Scn5q3PihB2Y9Bwo0CKnkYk7a7KT77EWv0Kkq+VHmOtqX3a2
hhX+b6a6ve9rzJ1qZYGj+obv/C3Sx1LzUjAfqVy7RJDf2uAoP5t2g8u/TkSpUxhM
VEjZBkSxYZhMyzQM6t8IgkUfnSrIPTHixbDWARZ4beMOBjxyPZK1nP7OOrNR3TkK

tD5BbnRocm9waWMgQ2xhdWRlIENvZGUgUmVsZWFzZSBTaWduaW5nIDxzZWN1cml0
eUBhbnRocm9waWMuY29tPokCUQQTAQoAOxYhBDHd3iTd+rZ59C170rqpKf8afsrO
BQJpyu9wAhsPBQsJCAcCAiICBhUKCQgLAgQWAgMBAh4HAheAAAoJELqpKf8afsrO

9n3CmZUmbynZ+E/QWNENpW/pDNZdWFy43PASmML5FHu4m9Sn
=oqMI
-----END PGP PUBLIC KEY BLOCK-----
CLAUDE_DESKTOP_PGP_EOF
  chmod 0644 "$APT_KEYRING"
}

write_apt_sources() {
  rm -f "$APT_SOURCES"  # see write_apt_keyring: don't write through a symlink
  # arch= constrains apt to the architectures the repo actually serves
  # (sign-linux-release.sh indexes only amd64 + arm64); without it a
  # multiarch system warns about every foreign arch on each `apt update`.
  cat > "$APT_SOURCES" <<EOF
$APT_SOURCES_MARKER
### Set CLAUDE_DESKTOP_ADD_REPO="true"|"false" in /etc/default/claude-desktop
### to force this entry on or off; remove this file too when opting out.
deb [arch=amd64,arm64 signed-by=/usr/share/keyrings/claude-desktop-archive-keyring.asc] $APT_REPO_URL $APT_SUITE $APT_COMPONENT
EOF
  chmod 0644 "$APT_SOURCES"

  # unattended-upgrades ignores repos whose Origin isn't allowlisted; ship
  # the snippet alongside an active entry so security-patch cadence covers
  # us too. Origins-Pattern with site= scopes the grant to this repo's host
  # — a bare "Anthropic:stable" Allowed-Origins entry would also enable
  # unattended installs from any other Anthropic-origin stable repo the
  # admin has configured. origin/archive must match the published
  # InRelease's Origin/Suite. Written/removed with the sources entry; apt
  # tolerates a snippet for a repo that isn't configured, so ordering is
  # not delicate.
  if [ -d "$ROOT/etc/apt/apt.conf.d" ]; then
    rm -f "$APT_UNATTENDED"
    cat > "$APT_UNATTENDED" <<EOF
$APT_SOURCES_MARKER
Unattended-Upgrade::Origins-Pattern {
	"origin=Anthropic,archive=stable,site=downloads.claude.ai";
};
EOF
    chmod 0644 "$APT_UNATTENDED"
  fi
}

configure_apt_repo() {
  # Not every dpkg system is an apt system (e.g. a stripped container image).
  # The sources.list.d directory is the gate; without it there is nowhere to
  # register the repo, and writing it would be noise.
  if [ ! -d "$ROOT/etc/apt/sources.list.d" ]; then
    return 0
  fi

  # Keyring is package-managed (/usr/share) and needed whether or not the
  # user keeps our sources entry, so write it unconditionally.
  write_apt_keyring

  # Read CLAUDE_DESKTOP_ADD_REPO from the default file. Parsed, not
  # sourced — in a DPKG_ROOT (chrootless) install this file lives inside
  # the target rootfs, and `.`-sourcing it would execute target content
  # as host root (and let it override APT_SOURCES/APT_KEYRING to host
  # paths). Only the exact values "true"/"false" are honoured.
  admin_override=""
  if [ -r "$APT_DEFAULT" ]; then
    # Tolerate leading whitespace, an optional `export` prefix, a trailing
    # `# comment`, and CRLF — common /etc/default idioms an admin would
    # reasonably expect to work.
    val=$(sed -n \
      's/^[[:space:]]*\(export[[:space:]]\{1,\}\)\{0,1\}CLAUDE_DESKTOP_ADD_REPO=\([^#]*\).*/\2/p' \
      "$APT_DEFAULT" | tail -n1 | tr -d '[:space:]')
    case "$val" in
      true  | '"true"'  | "'true'")  admin_override="true"  ;;
      false | '"false"' | "'false'") admin_override="false" ;;
      *) ;;  # unrecognised / unset → no override
    esac
  fi
  # An explicit admin opt-out means leave /etc/apt alone entirely.
  if [ "$admin_override" = "false" ]; then
    return 0
  fi

  # A sources file we didn't write (no marker on line 1) belongs to the
  # admin — leave it. A marker-owned file is rewritten active, which also
  # migrates installs from before the repo was live (their marker-owned
  # entry shipped commented out).
  if [ -e "$APT_SOURCES" ]; then
    first_line=$(head -n 1 "$APT_SOURCES" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$first_line" != "$APT_SOURCES_MARKER" ]; then
      return 0
    fi
  fi

  write_apt_sources
}

case "$1" in
  configure)
    # --- AppArmor userns profile ---------------------------------------------
    # Ubuntu 24.04+ ships kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1,
    # which makes CLONE_NEWUSER fail for unconfined processes; without this
    # profile Chromium falls back to the SUID chrome-sandbox helper (still
    # shipped 4755 as belt-and-suspenders). flags=(unconfined) does not
    # confine the app — it only allowlists it for userns. Same pattern as
    # Chrome / VS Code / 1Password.
    #
    # Gate on AppArmor 4.0 support: the abi/4.0 feature file only ships with
    # apparmor >= 4.0, so its presence means the parser can read this
    # profile. On AppArmor 3.x (Ubuntu 22.04, Debian 12) the profile is
    # unparseable and would be a permanent parse error for apparmor.service —
    # and grants nothing there anyway (the userns restriction is 24.04+
    # only). Those systems use the SUID chrome-sandbox fallback.
    #
    # Heredoc instead of a packaged /etc file: electron-installer-debian has
    # no extra-file staging, and a maintainer-script-owned file is rewritten
    # on every upgrade so it can't drift.
    if [ -f "$APPARMOR_DIR/abi/4.0" ]; then
      rm -f "$PROFILE"  # see write_apt_keyring: don't write through a symlink
      cat > "$PROFILE" <<EOF
abi <abi/4.0>,
include <tunables/global>

profile $PROFILE_NAME $BINARY flags=(unconfined) {
  userns,

  include if exists <local/$PROFILE_NAME>
}
EOF
      chmod 0644 "$PROFILE"
      # Load only when AppArmor is active and we're not in a DPKG_ROOT
      # cross-install; never fail the install on parser errors.
      if [ -z "$ROOT" ] \
         && command -v aa-enabled >/dev/null 2>&1 \
         && aa-enabled --quiet 2>/dev/null; then
        apparmor_parser -r -W -T "$PROFILE" || true
      fi
    fi

    # --- apt repo + keyring --------------------------------------------------
    configure_apt_repo

    write_search_provider
    ;;
  abort-upgrade|abort-remove|abort-deconfigure)
    ;;
  *)
    ;;
esac

exit 0
===== /tmp/cd/ctl/postrm
#!/bin/sh
# Debian maintainer script — runs as root via dpkg.
# Undoes what postinst wrote: the AppArmor userns profile, the apt
# keyring, the unattended-upgrades snippet, the GNOME search provider
# registration, and (if marker-owned) the sources entry. The package has no
# conffiles and never creates /etc/default/claude-desktop.
set -e

ROOT="${DPKG_ROOT:-}"

PROFILE="$ROOT/etc/apparmor.d/claude-desktop"
APT_KEYRING="$ROOT/usr/share/keyrings/claude-desktop-archive-keyring.asc"
APT_SOURCES="$ROOT/etc/apt/sources.list.d/claude-desktop.list"
APT_UNATTENDED="$ROOT/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/50claude-desktop"
SEARCH_PROVIDER_INI="$ROOT/usr/share/gnome-shell/search-providers/com.anthropic.Claude.search-provider.ini"
SEARCH_PROVIDER_SERVICE="$ROOT/usr/share/dbus-1/services/com.anthropic.Claude.SearchProvider.service"
# Keep in sync with postinst.
APT_SOURCES_MARKER="### Managed by the claude-desktop package."

remove_apparmor_profile() {
  if [ -f "$PROFILE" ]; then
    if [ -z "$ROOT" ] \
       && command -v aa-enabled >/dev/null 2>&1 \
       && aa-enabled --quiet 2>/dev/null; then
      apparmor_parser -R "$PROFILE" || true
    fi
    rm -f "$PROFILE"
  fi
}

remove_apt_repo() {
  action="$1"
  # The sources entry and keyring go together: the deb line is `signed-by=`
  # the keyring, so a dangling reference makes every `apt update` fail
  # verification. A markerless sources file is admin-owned (e.g. written by
  # the user following the help-center apt-first instructions, which
  # postinst then leaves alone) — on `remove`, keep the keyring so their
  # `apt update` keeps working; `purge` still cleans it.
  # /etc/default/claude-desktop is admin-created by construction and stays.
  if [ -e "$APT_SOURCES" ]; then
    first_line=$(head -n 1 "$APT_SOURCES" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$first_line" = "$APT_SOURCES_MARKER" ]; then
      rm -f "$APT_SOURCES"
    elif [ "$action" != "purge" ]; then
      # Admin-owned sources stay working: keep the keyring and the
      # unattended-upgrades snippet until purge.
      return 0
    fi
  fi
  rm -f "$APT_KEYRING" "$APT_UNATTENDED"
}

case "$1" in
  remove|purge)
    remove_apparmor_profile
    remove_apt_repo "$1"
    rm -f "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_INI" "$SEARCH_PROVIDER_SERVICE"
    # Per-user residue (~/.config/autostart entry, per-browser
    # NativeMessagingHosts manifests) is intentionally left — dpkg
    # maintainer scripts run as root and must not reach into user homes.
    ;;
  upgrade|failed-upgrade|abort-install|abort-upgrade|disappear)
    ;;
  *)
    ;;
esac

exit 0
